# Continued discovery iterations

User direction on 2026-10-08: “Iterate & discover until you get a desired output.” The previous [outcome](../OUTCOME.md) is a historical report of the first pass, not a completion claim for the resumed objective.

## Objective and success gate

Produce a genuinely new substantial theorem, or a substantial new case of a high-importance MathDB problem, through a nonroutine argument enabled by the accepted OpenAI corpus. Preserve the pinned premises and exact source contracts. A technically correct corollary, known reduction, unclosed conditional theorem, negative experiment, or new-to-project classification is not success.

Before accepting a candidate:

1. State its exact improvement over both accepted corpus and primary literature, with observed MathDB importance evidence.
2. Supply the new construction or estimate itself, with full hypotheses and quantifiers.
3. Seek counterexamples and independent proof review focused on the least justified steps.
4. Search primary prior art early and repeat the targeted check for the final statement.
5. Commit each validated substantive finding separately. Preserve failed arguments and corrections as research records, without inflating the finding count.

When a report returns without meeting the gate, integrate its evidence and use it to choose a concrete refinement or a fresh target. Do not automatically close the resumed research just because the current delegated batch finishes. Do not lower the novelty/significance standard to force success.

every quadrilateral and hexagon face by the odd-arc parity count — exact
translation bijections $N_{00}\to P_{ux}$ and $P_{vy}\to D_{11}$ give
$U=V+Z$ and $t\ge\ell-1$, with $b\equiv r+2z\pmod{L+U}$ and
equality-case failure exactly at $z\equiv-r/2$. **R170**: exact
boundary-residual generators
$\operatorname{diff}(J)=\operatorname{diff}(F_J)+\operatorname{span}(E_J)$,
deficit identity $\ell-t=\delta_V+\varepsilon-\delta_R$, exchange iff
$\delta_R+\eta\ge\delta_V+\varepsilon$. The blanket containments
$V\subseteq U$ and $V\subseteq L+U$ are refuted by certified octagonal
examples (indices 263, 272, 431; index 431 has explicit separator $w$
with $w\cdot U=0$, $w\cdot v=-8$), without touching the exchange target.

normalized weight $-\tfrac12$ of the disconnected square-switch
2-factor. The 1,120 tested quadrilateral steps have 792 equality occurrences,
all with

$t\le\ell-2$. Universal exchange, (T) and Q164 remain open. Parent
sealed replay passes (~131 s; seven payloads byte-equal; eight guard
probes blocked; drift-free context ledgers). The

## Iteration 07 — toward ten substantial findings

[R009, variable-channel four-state capacity](../findings/R009-variable-channel-capacity.md), is accepted after separate proof and final significance reviews. The independently audited locally finite strengthening belongs to the same finding. The current count is **3 accepted, 7 remaining**; the [count and continuation ledger](../TEN-FINDINGS.md) is authoritative.

The structural construction has passed proof review and is undergoing [final significance review](iteration7-structures-novelty-r3-task.json). The [completed complex-mode candidate](i10-complex-prime-modes.md) proposes to close CM and is undergoing [independent proof review](iteration10-complex-modes-r2-task.json). Earlier analysis and parent screens remain negative/reduction records; they add no findings.

### Capacity acceptance — 2026-10-08

The parent accepts R009 as the third substantial deduction under the pinned corpus. The contribution is the uniform local moment and flow-splitting sufficiency proof for arbitrary edge channels, including channels approaching or equal to perfect transmission. The final contribution audit recommends acceptance after checking variable-interaction Ising, Potts and robust-reconstruction literature and proposed standard reductions. Capacity formalism and the near-perfect ray example are not claimed as new.

The parent read the final reviews and reproduced the source, artifact and exact diagnostics: 41 pinned source files, 11 primary PDFs and 101 artifacts in the significance audit; five source files and 17 artifacts in the locally finite proof audit. The latter passes universal bounds, joint independence after zero-edge pruning and the infinite-branching counterexample. It also records a perfect-copy binary-gadget reduction of the qualitative locally finite statement to the new bounded-child theorem. These extensions do not merit another count. Worldwide priority remains unverified; no human peer review or formal certification is claimed.

### Complex-mode candidate integration — 2026-10-08

The parent read the full i10 proof proposal. It retains the nonzero Selberg–Delange principal terms in one position model shared by all active sets, proves exact small-label covariance, supplies separate discrete high-time and summed presieve estimates, and recombines the model contributions into positive proxy mass before taking absolute values. This would yield uniformity of Omega(p−1) modulo every fixed m jointly with the PD factor law in each fixed reduced progression. It is not yet accepted.

Parent checks reproduce all exact diagnostics byte for byte and verify 38 prior pinned sources, 11 fresh source copies, all input snapshots and 25 artifacts. The [parent record](i10-parent-preliminary-verification.json) limits these to finite algebra and provenance. A fresh independent reviewer must audit the rough density, aggregate square errors, physical residual, high-time scales, progression main terms, signed reassembly and limit order. No R-number or count increase precedes proof and final significance review.

### Analysis-screen integration

The [completed analysis report](i07-analysis.md) screens weak-MTW cut separation, constrained Ball–Evans approximation, and rough Calderón extensions. Stronger corpus companions already provide finite localization of the rough counterexample. The inverse W¹,¹ approximation transfer is routine, while the smooth compression example shows why forward approximation alone does not control any higher inverse exponent. The substantial missing construction is uniform integrability of the cofactor/negative-Jacobian energy or a derivative-controlled incompressibility correction. No such construction is claimed.

The parent read the full report and the forward approximation source contract, and verified all 60 pinned source blobs, six external retrievals and 81 author artifact hashes. Only the shared iteration index changed among snapshotted inputs. The [parent record](../sources/i07_analysis/parent-verification.json) explicitly limits this to source integrity and screening integration, not independent certification of the routine propositions. Preserve the negative/reduction report without an R-number or a count increase. The new variable-channel capacity candidate receives the next proof-review slot.

### Parity candidate integration — 2026-10-08

The candidate puts full Liouville on one physical endpoint and tracks the fixed selected-prime signs in each comparison pattern. It uses a nonempty long-prime tuple, classical Liouville distribution in ordinary progressions at low frequencies, and a coefficient-independent sparse Gram estimate at high frequencies. Its proposed extraction yields equal parity densities jointly with PD(1); finite square exclusions would then give joint squarefree independence and equal Möbius-sign densities. This is a new proof attempt, not a substitution into the source's band-limited sign theorem.

The parent reproduced the complete finite output and verified all 81 artifact hashes, 60 pinned manuscript files and six successful external downloads. The only changed input is this shared index. The [preliminary review](../sources/i05_parity_pd/parent-preliminary-review.md) records source-contract spot checks and their limits. A fresh independent analytic audit is running with the exact vulnerable steps specified in its brief. Decision: commit the candidate as a research checkpoint; do not promote it or increase the substantial-finding count before that review is integrated.

### Cross-field candidate integration — 2026-10-08

The scout rejected a known spectral thin-tree implication and a false entropy-to-subgaussian upgrade. Its positive candidate proves polynomial multiple pointwise convergence for common commuting Gaussian actions, including equal-degree polynomial maps, by conditioning independent Gaussian copies, controlling Wick contractions through spectral measures, and applying a classical single-function variation theorem. The final proof does not require the accepted corpus; this scope limitation is explicit.

The parent reproduced the full finite output, verified 107 upstream files, eight literature downloads and eight artifact hashes, and checked the actual variation theorem at p=2. The [preliminary review](../sources/i05_crossfield/parent-preliminary-review.md) records the scope. No accepted theorem or substantial-finding count is added. The parity audit and cubic-reservoir work continue independently.

The fresh independent audit passes CT, LC, SPR, signed prime extraction, size-biased tightness and every stated joint squarefree/Möbius consequence. It finds no unresolved analytic estimate. The parent read the complete audit and reproduced both finite suites. The independent review passes Theorem G, the finite-degree variation bound and the optional mixing limit, but demotes the contribution gate. The independent audit passes i05 Propositions 1–3 and retains the classical attribution of the Q/F/C criterion. ### Parity final acceptance — 2026-10-08

The fresh final review recommends accepting R008 as a substantial nonroutine deduction beyond R006. It screened the complete inventory and inspected stronger quantitative and qualitative multiplicative-correlation interfaces, the exact D/E/S contracts, and current primary prior art. It found no direct stronger theorem supplying LP and no new proof-critical problem. Carella's latest listed v3 explicitly makes its main scalar cancellation theorems conditional on a shifted-prime distribution hypothesis; this supersedes relying on v1 criticism alone. Shift-averaged and Siegel-zero-conditional results do not supply the fixed-predecessor joint theorem under these premises.

The review also proves the exact implication to Moree–Hommersom Conjecture 1: prescribe finitely many valuations, remove squares only outside that finite set, and apply LP in finitely many fixed classes before taking the square-tail cutoff to infinity. The parent checked the primary definition/conjecture and MathDB 377990 formulation. This is concrete named-problem relevance without an invented importance score or a separate finding count.

The parent read the full review, checked the decisive current-version statements, and verified 52 download hashes, 36 pinned Git blobs, 34 source ranges and 25 page extracts. Only this shared index had changed among recorded inputs before integration; see [verification](../sources/i05_parity_novelty_r3/parent-verification.json). R008 now contains the final scope, corrections and named consequence.

Decision: the second substantive-discovery objective is met by R008 on the qualified accepted-corpus basis. It is one finding, already committed separately for mathematical acceptance, with this final-review integration completing its significance assessment. Worldwide priority, independent validation of upstream truth, external peer review and formal certification are not claimed. Gaussian and cubic lanes remain respectively demoted and unclosed; neither is counted toward success. No further task is launched or publication attempted.

### I48 integration: planar stability is technical

Crucially, the operator’s ordinary spectral gap controls the mass omitted by the optimizing bipartition; generic near-bipartiteness alone would not. The resulting two groups have uniformly positive total mass, despite arbitrarily small individual facets. Averaging within each group chooses actual active normal directions. Angular mass transport and the linear support formula approximate an enclosing parallelogram at error O(sqrt(D/v)), where D is logarithmic curvature and v is centered slope variance.

At one interior time D is O(Δ). The mixed-area estimate gives endpoint distance O(sqrt(v+Δ)) to a homothetic pair. In the other branch, active normal widths, logarithmically affine parallelogram area and nonnegative endpoint area losses give distance O(sqrt(Δ+sqrt(D/v))) to a common-direction parallelogram pair. Balancing at v=Δ^(1/3) yields d𝓔 ≤ C Δ^(1/6), hence P10. Constants are uniform over facet count and arbitrary symmetric-body approximation. This intermediate improvement is a reduction certificate, not a separate substantial nomination or a sharpness claim; its inputs were available without the new OpenAI proof operations.

Independence of adjacent base coordinates does not establish independence from the full tangent history. Positive-noise results cannot simply be specialized to zero noise. ### I60 integration: technical slice transfer, scoped counterexamples, integral-cover gap

The [completed I60 report](i60-threshold-structural-combinatorics-discovery.md) screens four mechanisms and develops two actual slice constructions. No substantial finding is nominated. Its general transfer theorem T takes a Boolean invariant cube inequality Var ≤ a I at p=m/N to a uniform m-slice inequality Var ≤ [aN + E|Bin(N,p)−m|/2] δ_m, where δ_m is the probability that one uniform remove/add swap changes the Boolean function. Conditional completion preserves the mean on every layer; classical slice Poincaré pays the variance loss, a swap coupling pays the averaged L1 gradient, and coarea invokes the Boolean source inequality. The parent agrees that this is a technical transfer. It does not infer a real-valued spectral gap or noise sensitivity.

Consequently graph properties inherit the logarithmic-square bound using pivotal normalization Nδ_m on every slice, and the usual swap Dirichlet normalization on each fixed dense range, with its explicit density-dependent constant. Fixed-r hypergraphs inherit the source exponent r/(r−1). The second, random-priority construction has exact resampling and binary-tail identities, but the unchanged source capture lemma fails for multiple bits of a single edge. That method obstruction neither invalidates T nor requires a new theorem to repair the already closed technical target.

Three explicit examples reject the stronger proposals in their exact scopes. The two-edge wedge property has Var/I tending to 1/4, disproving the unnormalized logarithmic-square bound uniformly over sparse slices. A spanning perfect matching has integral expectation threshold below 2e/n while constant/n random graphs retain isolated vertices, so globally compatible injective piece embeddings cannot hold at a universal multiple of that threshold. For a two-root wedge, deleting the edges to common neighbors costs order np² at the root and kills every rooted copy, disproving the proposed resilience against every maximum-degree εnp deletion. The remaining candidate-vertex deletion allowance floor(nr^s/20) follows directly from unused source Hall slack and only embeds the active support; it is technical.

The unresolved target D asks for an absolute ε>0 such that every family of Bernoulli-p mass at least 1−ε has an integral cost-1/2 cover of the sets not contained in a union of two family members, at that same p. The source already gives two unions with a constant density loss, or 2^75 unions at the original density. I60 constructs the two-row signed Fourier certificate explicitly: its fractional cost is at most (1−p)(1−a)/a, where a is the family mass. This is attributed to Li's current v2 Theorem 1.15 and the source's credited two-row operation. Neither ordinary constant-factor rounding nor the source's high-power bin cover preserves both p and two unions. The range p≥1/2 is elementary through an antithetic coupling; the remaining target is unproved and unrefuted.

### I61 integration: a coefficient-extraction candidate enters independent review

The [completed I61 report](i61-averaged-entropy-coefficient-rounding.md) proposes CE61 at author level. For symmetric m-by-m coefficient matrices X_r with sum X_r² ≤ K I, fixed K, let t be the Gaussian squared curl of q(sum x_r X_r), divided by m. Then the squared coefficient-symmetry error plus the sum of squared commutators, divided by m, is at most C_K/log(e/t) for 0<t≤1/2, and vanishes at t=0. No prior coefficient symmetry, approximate normalization or density realization is assumed. This closes the previously stated bounded-energy extraction question if the proof survives review; it is not the full entropy theorem.

The proposed mechanism compares divided-difference equations at x and −x in one common eigenbasis. Strict convexity of the Mills function makes their coefficient differences have opposite signs, controlling cancellation and yielding coefficient symmetry and a polynomial curl estimate on a bounded spectral region. A projection acts on all three tensor slots. Gaussian second and fourth operator moments then pay its tails against arbitrary fixed tensor marginals, including the coefficient/input slot. The argument avoids a small Gaussian ball and a maximum-eigenvalue event. Optimizing the spectral cutoff yields the logarithmic rate. A separate spectral truncation of the symmetrized tensor pays any newly created heavy directions from its known distance to the originally capped coefficients; the resulting tensor retains a 4K energy cap.

I61 independently audits the original I59 covariance/Sobolev remainder, polar normalization, Gaussian Hodge identity, bounded-energy curl bound, channel identity, tensor-from-dephasing estimate, method obstructions and radial theorem, giving PASS ORIGINAL in their stated scopes. Those input verdicts do not independently validate CE61 itself. Its law-level application remains conditional on dimension-uniform algebra rounding and a fixed original coefficient cap. The new logarithmic rate also does not establish U's square-root rate.

The report develops two further constructions without claiming a solution. Channel spectral rounding gives an approximately rank-m projection but does not preserve complete positivity or a commutative algebra. An exact partial-trace projection stays a positive normalized distance from every dephasing map, demonstrating the failure of generic channel hypotheses; it is not a linked symmetric-tensor counterexample to AR. Variational deflation gives explicit one-step contracted-energy budgets, but their adaptive sum and the small-maximum branch remain unpaid. Original heavy-direction cross-slot mass, a single global orientation, and approximation with a uniform cap are still separate barriers.

### I62 integration: same-density partials and two explicit method failures

The [completed I62 report](i62-same-density-two-union-covers.md) leaves D **unproved and unrefuted**. Its independent input audit passes I60's original two-row fractional certificate without repairs, with Li's current theorem fully attributed. Downward closure preserves the exceptional family, which becomes the induced non-two-colorability event for the hypergraph of minimal forbidden sets. The actual Talagrand text already contains the downward-closure observation; the reformulation is not a new contribution.

For forbidden rank at most r and independent-set probability a>0, I62 proves an integral cover of the entire complement with original-density cost at most (2^r−1)((2−p)^r−1)(1−a)/a. Finite LP duality, a second moment and bounded-row rounding prove this directly. A one-threshold rounding gives cost at most 6(1−a)/a for graphs, so a≥12/13 suffices. These are classical technical results that do not use a new corpus mechanism; unbounded rank remains unpaid. A disjoint-block, single-core case also follows directly from product independence with cost at most −log a.

A second construction chooses separated edge-pair unions from a random vertex order. The actual Pluhár/Lovász argument, as presented by Ferber–Shapira, proves that every ordering gives an integral cover. High independence probability does not control its raw cost. A book of m triangles has independence probability tending to one when p tends to zero, yet the construction selects all triangles at cost mp³. Its exact optimal cost is min(p²,mp³), because one shared edge covers every triangle. Thus even pruning to true critical cores does not replace overlap compression.

The spectral construction has a different, fully realized obstruction. For every fixed amplification C>1, I62 builds connected uniform forbidden hypergraphs with independent-set probability tending to one, a nonempty exceptional family, and divergent enlarged-density cost of their actual squared Fourier coefficients. The divergence survives retaining only odd supports. Their unique critical core has optimal original-density cost p^(2r+1), tending to zero: these examples refute that coefficient-budget estimate, not D. A separate five-point cardinality downset has fractional cost below one half and exact integral cost above one half, but its probability is below one half. It is likewise not a high-mass counterexample. The report distinguishes these failures from the remaining possibility of selecting only useful supports and compressing overlapping cores.

### Delayed I29 integration: sharp common-base entropy is technical only

The [completed I29 report](i29-common-base-entropy.md), returned after the later discovery rounds, proves at author level the rank-sharp inequality H_C(x) ≥ F(x)−(1−1/r)B(x) for every feasible mean x of the common bases of two labelled rank-r matroids, r≥1. Here H_C is the maximum entropy of a realizing law, F=−Σx_i log x_i, and B=−Σ(1−x_i)log(1−x_i). Rank zero is treated separately. The Gibbs and weighted partition-function consequences retain that maximum-entropy qualifier; the statement is false for arbitrary laws with the same mean.

The parent agrees with the technical-only disposition after reading the full report and both decisive source operations. In the polymatroid source, the tight rank normals of each matroid are already spanned by a chain. At strictly fractional coordinates each chain has at most r members and the two spans share the total-set normal, giving active rank at most 2r−1. The matching entropy source's finite-support covariance trace argument then applies directly: at a positive defect maximum it gives 0 ≥ d−n+(1+c)r. Contraction/deletion of coordinates equal to one/zero and a strict perturbation c=1−1/R+ε produce the contradiction on every boundary face. Even-cycle partition pairs attain the coefficient, so sharpness adds no new separator mechanism. No hard-intersection log-concavity or R011 graph analogy is assumed.

The second route makes the compatibility gap explicit. A set tight in both matroids splits the common-base face as a product, but a set tight in only one requires a law on entire interface bases and conditional completion matroids. A four-element partition pair shows that independent marginal gluing produces invalid bases with probability one half, that the common-base polynomial need not be log-concave, and that a particular all-common-basis root reserve has unbounded required coefficient. These statements refute those exact shortcuts, not every possible rooted or separator inequality. Current primary capacity comparisons and their stability/Lorentzian hypotheses remain attributed.

### I66 integration: logarithmic robustness of periodic blowup enters review

The [completed I66 report](i66-dispersive-equation-discovery.md) screens three distinct targets. The broad variable-metric three-dimensional wave L³ full-gain extension conflicts with the cited positive-curvature obstruction; no arbitrarily small metric-neighborhood claim is inferred. The proposed singular-measure Schrödinger endpoint remains unresolved: the available packet exponent and Lebesgue-area localization do not supply the required fractal endpoint estimate. Neither screen is nominated as a finding.

C66 changes the periodic defocusing equation itself to i u_t+Δu=|u|^(p−1)u(1+κ/log(2+|u|²)) on T¹². The author claims that for a sufficiently large odd p, k=⌈3p/4⌉, and all sufficiently small real |κ|, a nonempty H^k-open set still blows up at the source Schrödinger scale, with the same positive limiting normalized center amplitude. The source profile, spectrum, expanding-torus transfer and original degree mechanism remain attributed. This is a proposed complete deduction, not independent proof approval or a ninth accepted finding.

The proposed new argument first sharpens the source pressure bound to 17/50, allowing the needed fixed Sobolev order to satisfy k+3<p. It then normalizes the amplitude by c_κ(σ)=(1+κ/(2aσ))^(−a), with σ=log(1/(T−t)). This cancels the leading logarithmic perturbation and is claimed to leave a full C^(k+3) nonlinear forcing of order |κ|/σ², including inputs near zero and the expanding spatial tail. A uniform composition argument retains the periodic constant mode. The stable and positive spectral coordinates use σ² weights, while the phase uses σ. A quantitative defect bound for the phase column follows from gauge covariance; it does not assign a rate to the source's general coordinate defect, which only tends to zero. The final degree radius is chosen as d/S to keep all stable arguments inside the graph domain and obtain an actual open physical neighborhood.

This defeats that proposed positivity estimate; it does not bound trace distance before partial transpose or exclude a different Hilbert–Schmidt repair. The strongest target RP asks whether the source's strict volume and pairwise capacity inequalities suffice in real dimension at least six when arbitrary finitely many ball embeddings are already prescribed and must remain exactly fixed. A direct complement approach encounters a genuine limitation of the proposed comparison: every invariant probability measure of the chosen rearrangement must have positive potential average. Requiring it to fix a protected negative-potential region can violate this despite positive total mean and positive outer collar. This refutes that relative comparison, not RP. For two base covectors, the top-degree positivity condition contains a Poisson bracket, which small function amplitudes alone do not control. The source already avoids this with a carefully arranged single base covector; nontoric dependence alone is not its gap. A new relative model must preserve the protected balls, outer moments, collars and positivity throughout the transport.

The strongest unconditional partial packs arbitrary sufficiently small finite balls into any bounded open complement at a **fixed positive volume slack**. Finite disjoint internal round balls cover all but part of that slack; strict next-fit allocation and the stipulated source packing theorem fill these bins. The small-capacity threshold depends on the actual complement and the slack. This does not prove a threshold uniform as slack tends to zero or extension of arbitrary admissible larger balls. These technical partials have author arguments; they have not received independent analytic and contribution acceptance.

### I73 integration: thin polynomial twists and two missing statistical estimates

The [completed arithmetic-geometry report](i73-arithmetic-geometry-discovery.md) screens three targets: minimal analytic-rank statistics for a polynomial twist family, a Selmer converse for arbitrary non-CM elliptic curves over imaginary quadratic fields, and a polynomial family-uniform height bound for non-CM elliptic-square points on curves in A₂. All remain open. The rational-curve converse, full BSD from low corank, fixed simultaneous twist selection, finite-rank translated abelian ZP and the qualitative extension to complex fields are already covered by inspected source or classical operations. No substantial finding is nominated.

The pursued target fixes E/Q of conductor N and a positive M divisible by 8N, then samples positive n for which d=M²n²+1 is squarefree. The source root-number formula fixes parity b=(1−w(E))/2. The proposed minimal-rank density requires only o(X) sampled indices above b; the stronger analytic mean requires the total rank excess to be o(X). The squarefree sample has positive density in n but zero density in d. A source exceptional set of size o(Y) may contain every such polynomial value up to Y. A sparse abstract countermodel makes this failure explicit; it is not an elliptic-curve counterexample. The rational double cover proves generic function-field rank zero, but does not control specialization ranks.

Construction I derives the exact local character law, including its squarefree-conditioned version, and a periodic Gram comparison. At odd p not dividing M, the unconditioned first moment is −1/p; at primes dividing M the character is identically one. Thus the source's symmetric integer model cannot simply be reused. The elementary Gram error is at most 2X⁻¹(Σm|a_m|)², giving a short range L≤X^(1/3−ε) for fixed positive ε and divisor-bounded normalized coefficients. The source comparison needs lengths at least X when twist size Y is comparable to X². A polynomial-family estimate for the actual long coefficients, biased-model mollification, weighted squarefree removal and terminal analytic tail remain unpaid. Improving a short-range bound alone would not prove minimal-rank density.

Construction II uses the actual higher-Selmer convention r_k=s+#{finite cyclic exponents at least k}. It identifies a sufficient missing estimate: the upper density of r_k>b must tend to zero as k tends to infinity, with the large-X limit taken first. Finite 2-Selmer size is not full corank, and pointwise convergence of the levels does not justify exchanging limits. The inspected Koymans–Smith polynomial-twist theorem gives algebraic-rank exponential moments through finite-isogeny minimization; its sieve leaves large-prime entries uncontrolled. It does not imply minimal corank or analytic-rank integrability. Even minimal-corank density would leave the analytic mean on exceptional indices unpaid: a stronger o(X/log X) exceptional count or a separate analytic uniform-integrability bound would suffice. These transfer implications are technical reductions, not completed statistical theorems.

### I72 integration: rare girth conditioning and spectral input gaps

The [completed graph-probability report](i72-graph-probability-discovery.md) screens fixed-energy GOE universality under logarithmic-girth conditioning, nonstationary long-edge percolation robustness, and fixed-strength bounded-activity switch mixing. The strongest target samples a uniform labelled simple fixed-d graph conditional on girth greater than max(3, floor(.01 log_(d−1)n)), at a fixed interior bulk energy with the source Kesten–McKay normalization. It remains **unproved and unrefuted**; no substantial finding is nominated.

MWW's relative short-cycle count gives conditioning probability exp(−Λ_g+o(1)), with Λ_g of order n^.01/log n. Thus even failure probabilities smaller than every fixed inverse power do not transfer by division. Construction I proves exact marked finite-switch reversibility with rejection at most C(d,k)(d−1)^g/n, and a conditional type-count estimate that pays log(1/q) as an added entropy cost. Neither requires constrained-chain mixing. The unpaid input is the clean source's complete spectral event within the conditioned law: rigidity, complete delocalization, and microscopic-height trace/star estimates. The actual Huang–Yau bootstrap uses an exact marked measure map and polynomially many vertex/grid tests. A small additive rejection error per local resampling does not survive those union bounds without further control. Disconnected repeated high-girth examples refute a deterministic girth-only replacement, not the random-ensemble target.

Construction II removes cycles from an ordinary random regular graph. MWW's average forward/backward switch counts determine cardinality ratios, not a uniform final law; a two-by-two incidence example distinguishes these. Even a correct output-law correction would leave microscopic spectral cancellation unpaid. Rank bounds for n^.01 changes give a macroscopic distribution estimate but no vanishing microscopic resolvent difference. A genuine K3,3-to-prism switch changes the zero multiplicity, confirming the rank-only limitation without refuting the random target. Cycle-selected endpoints also cannot be treated as uniform sampled edges. The two required estimates—total-variation output law TL and distributional spectral comparison SC—remain separate gaps.

The weak-Anderson companion supplies spectral averaging and compression bounds for any real symmetric graph matrix independent of the potentials, so graph-only conditioning preserves that input. Its constants depend on positive disorder strength. Fixed positive disorder does not imply the clean limit: microscopic perturbation scale is nw and the source takes n to infinity at fixed w. A direct positive-disorder conditioned proof must still pay the empirical recursion on the same realized graph, environment-conditioned entropy, centering, tails and all later limiting operations.
